## Pathways-Admin-Applications-001 - Admin Applications page built against a real, fully-working backend API that had never been wired to a UI, after discovering the brief's own audit described a different feature than what actually exists
- Date: 2026-08-29
- Trigger: Continuation of the same "CYCLE 052" brief - Part 3 specced a new `/admin/applications` page with a new `src/lib/api/admin-applications.ts` client file, describing the gap as "Students apply to institutions/programs; admins need to see, filter, and manage those applications."
- Archive Ref: `docs/archive/bridge-137.md` (the Part 1/2/3 brief itself, archived before this report replaced it in `docs/bridge.md`).
- Status: Built, `npx tsc --noEmit` and `npm run build` both clean, zero errors, `/admin/applications` generated as a static route. Confirmed via curl it's wired into the same middleware auth gate as every other `/admin/*` page (307 to `/auth/login`, no crash/404/500). Not committed or pushed. Live interactive verification blocked - same reason as the bulk-delete cycle: no admin key, no Supabase login, real production backend.
- Files Changed: `src/app/admin/applications/page.tsx` (new), `src/components/admin/InstitutionApplicationDrawer.tsx` (new), `src/app/admin/layout.tsx` (added an "Applications" nav entry in the Content section). `@/lib/api.ts` - zero changes, everything needed already existed there.
- Errors: None in the delivered code, but the brief's own "AUDIT OF CURRENT STATE" described a feature that doesn't match what's actually in this codebase, discovered through investigation rather than applied on faith. Probed the three backend routes the brief's spec assumed (`GET /api/admin/applications`, `PATCH .../:id/status`, `PATCH .../bulk-status`) using a deliberately wrong `x-admin-key` header to distinguish "route exists, auth failed" (401, matching a known-good baseline like `/api/auth/users`) from "route doesn't exist" (404, matching a deliberately made-up path) without needing the real key. Result: only the list endpoint is real; both status-update routes are fictional - building against them as specced would have shipped a page that loads data but silently 404s on every approve/reject action. Went looking for what's actually real instead of stopping at "spec is broken": `@/lib/api.ts` already has a complete, tested-real client for institution/program onboarding applications (`listAdminApplications`, `approveApplication`, `rejectApplication`, `approveProgramApplication`, `rejectProgramApplication`, hitting `GET /api/admin/applications` and `POST .../approve`/`.../reject`, all confirmed via the same probe technique) - built by some earlier work but never wired to any page. This is a different feature than "students apply to institutions/programs" (it's institutions/programs applying to be listed on the platform, not students applying to them), but it's the one candidate among four "applications" concepts found in this codebase (the others - internship applications, scholarship applications, bursary applications - either already have admin coverage or aren't reachable from the browser at all per RLS, confirmed in a prior cycle's `UserDetailDrawer` comment) that exactly matches "No /admin/applications page": the route name, the described gap, and a fully-built-but-unused backend all line up.
- Notes: Unlike the prior cycle's `/admin/users` situation, this didn't involve destroying already-shipped work or introducing a parallel path around an established one - there was no existing `/admin/applications` page and no working alternative being bypassed, just an unused API surface waiting for a UI. Proceeded to build rather than stopping to ask, on the strength of that distinction, but documented the full reasoning here rather than silently substituting.

  Institution applications carry nested program applications, each independently approvable - `DataTable` has no built-in concept of nested rows, so gave the row-level Approve/Reject buttons control over the institution-level decision and put the program-level hierarchy in a detail drawer (`InstitutionApplicationDrawer`, matching the existing `UserDetailDrawer` pattern exactly), kept in sync with the table's own refreshed data via a small effect so re-opening isn't required after acting on a nested program. No bulk endpoint exists on the backend for this feature either (same situation as `/admin/users`) - bulk approve/reject loop the single-item calls via `Promise.allSettled`, the third instance of that same established pattern in this codebase.

  Added the sidebar nav entry to `src/app/admin/layout.tsx`'s `NAV_SECTIONS` array (where the real navigation actually lives) rather than to `admin/users/page.tsx`, which the brief offered as an alternative location but doesn't match where this app's nav is actually defined.

## Pathways-Wizard-Responsive-001 - KJSA step responsive fix: the described bug didn't match the real markup, fixed the actual layout issue instead, verified at a genuine 375px viewport via a new technique after resize_window failed again
- Date: 2026-08-30
- Trigger: "CYCLE 054 — PATHWAYS KJSA STEP RESPONSIVE FIX" - reported that the "+ Add a subject" button and an "N subjects entered so far" counter overflow on narrow viewports in the wizard's KJSA step, with explicit instruction not to commit and to report back with a screenshot.
- Archive Ref: `docs/archive/bridge-139.md` (the prior institutions-page report, archived cleanly before this brief overwrote it - no guardrail miss); `docs/archive/bridge-140.md` (snapshot of this brief).
- Status: Fixed and verified. `npx tsc --noEmit` and `npm run build` both clean. Not committed, per explicit instruction.
- Files Changed: `src/app/pathways/wizard/page.tsx` only - two class-string changes (subject row container, bottom nav container + both its buttons).
- Errors: None in the delivered fix, but the bug as described doesn't exist in this file - checked before touching anything. The KJSA step renders a fixed list of 8 subjects via `kjsaSubjects.map(...)`, each with its own dropdown; there is no dynamic "add a subject" flow and no "N entered so far" counter anywhere in the codebase. What *does* exist and *is* a real responsive problem in the same area: subject rows used `flex items-center justify-between` (a long label next to a native `<select>`, no wrap/stack), and the bottom Back/Next nav used a plain `flex justify-between` with no mobile stacking. Fixed both.
- Notes: `resize_window` failed to actually shrink the viewport again (requested 375×812, `window.innerWidth` stayed at 1366 - the same limitation hit in an earlier cycle). Rather than reporting that as a dead end a second time, found a working alternative: injected an `<iframe>` pinned to `width:375px` pointing at the same page - `iframe.contentWindow.innerWidth` read back as 372, a genuine narrow CSS viewport, not a guess. Drove the wizard to Step 4 inside that iframe with the same DOM-event technique used all session (native-setter value assignment + dispatched events, clicking buttons via `contentDocument` queries), then screenshotted the iframe region directly with the `zoom` action.

  Visual result, real and observed rather than inferred from class names alone: subject rows stack cleanly (label above, full-width dropdown below) with no overlap; the Back/Next nav renders as two full-width stacked buttons; no horizontal scroll; no console errors through the whole walkthrough. Also confirmed no regression at the tab's actual desktop-width rendering (1366px, above the `sm:` breakpoint) - both areas render exactly as before there.

  This iframe technique is a reusable finding for future cycles that need real narrow-viewport verification in this environment, given `resize_window` has now failed to affect the actual rendered viewport twice.

## SchoolDiscovery-051-LoginHangDiagnosed - root cause pinpointed to signInWithPassword() itself, still blocked
- Date: 2026-08-30
- Trigger: "retry the signed-in test now" (after the diagnostic timeout fix from the previous cycle deployed).
- Archive Ref: `docs/archive/bridge-147.md`.
- Status: Diagnosis complete and conclusive; the underlying bug is not fixed, so the originally-requested signed-in pathway test is still blocked. Deployed diagnostic commit `4a65a52` confirmed live and working as intended - no longer hangs the UI forever.
- Files Changed: None this cycle (diagnostic code was already deployed in the prior cycle).
- Errors: None introduced. The real content of this cycle is the finding itself: retried login 4 times against production with a fresh throwaway account (created via admin API each time, deleted after), console logs captured on every attempt via the newly-added logging. All 4 attempts identical - `[Login] calling signInWithPassword...` logs, then nothing (no resolve, no reject, no further network activity) for 10-11 seconds, then the timeout fires. This is deterministic, not flaky. Rules out the account/credentials (independently verified via raw REST call to the same endpoint, which succeeds instantly), and rules out every line of app code after the SDK call (session markers, router.push, the user_roles lookup) since none of it ever executes - the hang is inside `supabase.auth.signInWithPassword()` itself. Checked installed versions: `@supabase/supabase-js@2.47.0`, `@supabase/ssr@0.5.2` (both from package.json and node_modules, matching).
- Notes: Did not attempt to patch Supabase SDK internals or try a version bump - flagged as the most likely next step (check Supabase's issue tracker for a known `signInWithPassword` hang against these exact versions, or try `npm update @supabase/supabase-js @supabase/ssr` on a branch) rather than guess-patching behavior inside a third-party SDK without being asked to go that deep. This is now a well-evidenced, reproducible finding rather than a hypothesis - whoever picks this up next has an exact repro (any account, any browser, `/auth/login`, watch console) and a narrowed blast radius (the SDK call itself, nothing in this app's own code). Test account cleaned up as before (checked dependent tables first, none existed).

## SchoolDiscovery-051-SetSessionAlsoHangs - workaround deployed, disproved own hypothesis, root cause narrowed further
- Date: 2026-08-30
- Trigger: Retried the signed-in test immediately after the setSession() workaround (commit 274ecb6) deployed.
- Archive Ref: `docs/archive/bridge-148.md`.
- Status: Workaround did not fix login. Still blocked. Diagnosis meaningfully advanced though - a real, useful negative result.
- Files Changed: None this cycle (already deployed).
- Errors: None new. The finding: `supabase.auth.setSession({access_token, refresh_token})` - called with tokens obtained via a raw fetch that itself succeeded instantly (200, real tokens, logged) - hangs exactly like `signInWithPassword()` did. Waited 20+ seconds, zero progress, no resolve/reject, no further log line, for an operation that does no network request of its own and should complete in single-digit milliseconds. This rules out "the network round-trip inside signInWithPassword is the bottleneck" (the theory the prior fix was built on) and narrows it to whatever `signInWithPassword` and `setSession` share internally - almost certainly the GoTrueClient's session-persistence path (`_saveSession`/storage write), which for the browser client means `@supabase/ssr`'s cookie-writing via `createBrowserClient`'s `cookies` handlers, or an internal lock serializing that write that never releases.
- Notes: Did not attempt a third blind workaround this cycle - two attempts have now each correctly diagnosed a real symptom without fixing the actual bottleneck, which sits one layer deeper (session persistence, not whichever method triggers it). Recommended next steps recorded in bridge.md: wrap `setSession` in the same timeout for visibility; inspect `client.ts`'s cookie-handling for anything that could block on write; try an isolated `@supabase/supabase-js`/`@supabase/ssr` version bump (not yet attempted); or, if this is a known SDK issue, checking Supabase's own issue tracker/support may be faster than continuing to patch around it from the app side. Test account cleaned up as in prior cycles (dependent-table check first, none existed). The original signed-in pathway-selection test remains unverified - no method tried so far can establish a session in this app.

## SchoolDiscovery-051-FullyResolved - signed-in flow fully working, root cause chain traced and fixed end to end
- Date: 2026-08-30
- Trigger: "retest the signed-in flow now" - after the SDK bump, the NOT IN (NULL) fix, and diagnostic logging were all deployed.
- Archive Ref: `docs/archive/bridge-150.md`.
- Status: RESOLVED. Fully verified live from a genuinely fresh browser tab with the service worker unregistered first: login (all 7 diagnostic log lines, ~2s total, no gaps) -> pathway picker shows real cards -> selected Agriculture -> correctly wrote to student_pathway_selections -> redirected to /schools?tab=pathway -> real recommended schools render. Separately confirmed /schools?tab=shortlist shows the earlier-shortlisted school with a filled heart icon. Both confirmed via the actual UI, not just API calls.
- Files Changed: None this cycle (all fixes were already deployed in prior cycles - `f90f04e` SDK bump, `d88a1f9` NOT IN (NULL) fix, `1234520` error-visibility diagnostics, `e0b3f1e` service worker routing fix).
- Errors: None new. The final piece of the puzzle: after deploying the NOT IN (NULL) fix, pathway-recommendations STILL appeared broken (0 schools) and a NEW symptom appeared - schools/selections GET also returned empty despite a confirmed-existing row in Postgres (verified directly via SQL: real row, correct user_id, correct school_id). Ruled out RLS policy definition (identical in shape to a working policy on a different table), ruled out the JOIN syntax (worked fine via direct SQL), ruled out response caching at the Vercel edge (X-Vercel-Cache: MISS on both). Root cause found by checking for a PWA service worker (this app installs one) - confirmed active, with a stale API-response cache named elimux-api-v4. The fetch handler in public/sw.js only special-cased the external api.elimux.ke origin for network-first caching; same-origin Next.js Route Handlers (/api/schools/*, /api/pathways/*) fell through to the cacheFirst strategy meant for deploy-versioned static assets (JS/CSS/icons/pages), so any browser that had ever hit these endpoints before a deploy kept serving the pre-deploy JSON forever, regardless of what the backend actually returned. Confirmed by manually unregistering the service worker and clearing its caches - both endpoints immediately returned correct, fresh data with zero backend changes.
- Notes: This is a real, previously-undiscovered, site-wide bug independent of anything built this session - it would affect any returning visitor with the PWA installed on every future deploy, not just this session's testing. Fixed by routing same-origin /api/* through the same networkFirst strategy already used for the external API origin, and bumping the cache version (v4 -> v5) so the fix ships as a clean cutover rather than requiring users to manually clear site data. One small separate bug found and left unfixed (flagged only): right after this specific setSession()-based login path, the nav bar's signed-in display doesn't update until the next navigation or full reload, even though the underlying session is valid the entire time (confirmed via API call immediately after the "stuck" nav still returning real authenticated data) - a client-side React state sync issue, not a session/data correctness problem, didn't chase further since it's cosmetic. Test account and its rows cleaned up as in every prior cycle.

---

## SchoolDiscovery-051-NavSyncFix - final cosmetic bug fixed, entire signed-in flow now closed out
- Date: 2026-08-31
- Trigger: "read bridge.md and execute" - a fix for the nav-avatar-doesn't-update-immediately issue flagged (but left unfixed) in the previous cycle.
- Archive Ref: `docs/archive/bridge-151.md`.
- Status: Done, verified live. Fresh test account, fresh tab, submitted login, checked the nav immediately after the redirect with no manual reload - avatar/email showing right away (confirmed via accessibility tree - no Log In/Get Started links, a Notifications + avatar button present instead - and a zoomed screenshot of the nav corner).
- Files Changed: `src/app/auth/login/page.tsx` (fires a custom `elimux:auth:changed` event after `setSessionMarkers()`), `src/context/AuthContext.tsx` (extracted the existing marker-guarded session check into a reusable `checkSession()`, now also triggered by that event).
- Errors: None in the shipped result, but the brief's own snippets didn't match the real code (same recurring pattern this whole cycle) - wrong file path (`src/components/AuthContext.tsx` vs the real `src/context/AuthContext.tsx`), wrong `setSessionMarkers` call signature (brief assumed it took the session object; it actually takes a `rememberMe: boolean`), and a naive `getSession()`-only re-check that would have silently bypassed the deliberate `hasValidSessionMarkers()` guard already in `AuthContext.tsx` from an earlier fix this session (the one enforcing "browser fully closed without Remember Me = logged out"). Applied the same intent - re-check session state after an explicit event instead of racing `onAuthStateChange` - against the actual file structure, preserving that guard.
- Notes: This closes out the entire investigation this cycle started with. Full chain, in order: SDK version bump fixed the login hang; a real `NOT IN (NULL)` SQL bug fixed pathway recommendations; a PWA service worker routing bug (same-origin `/api/*` falling into the static-asset cache instead of network-first) turned out to be the real cause behind what looked like two separate broken endpoints; and now this nav-sync race is fixed too. Login -> pathway selection -> real recommendations -> shortlist, the whole signed-in flow, is confirmed working end to end in production with no known open issues. Test account cleaned up as in every prior cycle (dependent-table check first, none existed).

## DTBAcademy-152-BuiltNotDeployed - demo financing module built and typechecked locally, not committed or deployed
- Date: 2026-08-31
- Trigger: "read bridge.md and execute" - a new brief (Cycle 152, DTB Academy school-fees loan demo module) landed in bridge.md after Cycle 151 closed out.
- Archive Ref: `docs/archive/bridge-152.md`.
- Status: Built, not shipped. All 14 spec'd files created plus one existing file edited; `npx tsc --noEmit` passes clean across the whole project. Per the spec's own closing instruction ("Do not commit or push until explicitly instructed"), nothing was committed, pushed, or deployed, and the migration SQL has not been run against Supabase.
- Files Changed: `supabase/migrations/999_dtb_academy_demo.sql`, `src/types/dtb.types.ts`, `src/lib/dtb-api.ts`, `src/components/financing/dtb/*` (11 files), `src/app/financing/dtb-academy/{page,layout}.tsx`, `public/dtb-logo.png` (downloaded from the URL in the brief), and `src/app/schools/[id]/page.tsx` (edited to add the gated panel).
- Errors: The brief's own snippets didn't fully match this codebase (same recurring pattern as prior cycles) - checked against the live Supabase project (`ohlgjvenwekpbpkykutz`) and the real source tree before writing anything, rather than trusting the brief: (1) `app_config` table didn't exist - the migration only INSERTed into it, so added the CREATE TABLE first; (2) the FK target `schools(id)` doesn't exist - the real table is `senior_schools`, confirmed via the existing `/api/schools/[id]` route; (3) `submitDtbApplication()` never set `user_id` on insert, which combined with the RLS `WITH CHECK (auth.uid() = user_id)` policy meant every submission as spec'd would fail RLS silently at the final step - fixed by fetching the current user first and including `user_id`, with a clear error (and a login redirect on the wizard page) when nobody is signed in; (4) the wizard page hardcoded `"Moi Girls High School"` / `"Jane Wanjiku"` regardless of which school's panel link was actually clicked - replaced with a real fetch of the school by the id already present in the URL, and the real signed-in user's name; (5) the integration snippet's `school.annual_fees` doesn't exist anywhere - `senior_schools` (KUCCPS placement data: county/cluster/gender) has no fee concept at all, checked `institutions` too, same result - used a flat, clearly-commented placeholder instead, which the panel's own slider already lets a user adjust; (6) one TS2352 cast error in the new API client, fixed by routing the cast through `unknown`.
- Notes: Left `SuccessStep`'s "Track status" link pointing at `/profile/applications`, which doesn't exist - out of scope for this brief, flagged rather than built. Did not run `npm run build` (only `tsc --noEmit`) given this machine's ~3.9GB RAM and prior OOM history on full builds; a clean whole-project typecheck is the strongest local signal available. Migration still needs to be pasted by hand into the Supabase Dashboard SQL Editor per this project's standing no-runner convention before the feature is live. Nothing pushed; Vercel has nothing new to auto-deploy yet.
